```text
prompt=Check this API payload for errors. List all validation issues:

assistant=starting

1. **`email`**: `"john@.com"` is invalid. Standard email formats do not allow the domain portion to start with a period (`.`).
2. **`age`**: `200` is out of range. Human age realistically falls between `0` and `120` (or `150`), making `200` an invalid value.
3. **`phone`**: `"555-12"` is malformed. Phone numbers typically require a minimum of 7-10 digits depending on the region/country code, and this format is too short and structurally incorrect.
4. **`date`**: `"2020-13-45"` is invalid. Month `13` does not exist (valid range: `01-12`), and day `45` exceeds the maximum days in any month.
5. **`amount`**: `-50` is invalid. Monetary amounts should be non-negative (`>= 0`). Negative values typically indicate a debt or refund, which should be handled via a separate `balance` or `transaction_type` field rather than a raw `amount`.

3. `phone`: "555-12" - Invalid phone number format. Usually requires 10 digits (US) or 7-12 digits depending on country. "555-12" is too short and malformed.
4. `date`: "2020-13-45" - Invalid date. Month 13 does not exist. Day 45 does not exist.
5. `amount`: -50 - Invalid amount. Monetary amounts are typically non-negative (>= 0).

- Amount: Invalid value (negative monetary amount).

2. **`age`**: `200` is out of range. Human age realistically falls between `0` and `120` (or `150`), making `200` an invalid value.

4. **`date`**: `"2020-13-45"` is invalid. Month `13` does not exist (valid range: `01-12`), and day `45` exceeds the maximum days in any month.
5. **`amount`**: `-50` is invalid. Monetary amounts should be non-negative (`>= 0`). Negative values typically indicate a debt or refund, which should be handled via a separate `balance` or `transaction_type` field rather than a raw `amount`.
```

```text
Key points to include:

- Frontend: Dashboard components delivered ahead of schedule.
- Backend: ETL pipeline parallelized, processing time reduced from 45 to 12 mins.
- Bugs fixed: timezone conversion, race condition, CSV export.
- Schema migration v3 to v4: users table gains mfa_enabled, last_password_change; orders table partitioned; products table many-to-many relationship.
- HTTP client retry mechanism concerns: needs jitter, configurable max retries, distinguish transient vs permanent errors. Suggested RetryPolicy class.
- Transformer architecture: RoPE improves length generalization. Flash attention v2 reduces memory.
- PostgreSQL 14 to 16 migration: scheduled for last weekend of September, staged rollout.
- Event-driven architecture: Apache Kafka, schema registry, DLQ alerts.
- API rate limiting: tiered limits, sliding window algorithm, 429 status codes.
- Incident INC-4821: memory leak fixed, deployed version 2.14.4.
- REST API endpoint POST /v2/analyses details.
- Distributed caching layer details.
- User engagement metrics: 12% increase, bounce rate 34%, tutorial retention 67%.
- Security audit: bcrypt cost 10->12, JWT expiration 24h->1h, CORS policy, request signing, progressive delays on login, session invalidation.

I will summarize these into a concise report email.
Let's draft it.
Subject: Project Update Report
To: Sarah
Body: Hi Sarah, Please find below the latest project update report covering the recent sprint retrospective, schema migrations, infrastructure updates, and security audit findings. [Summary of points] Let me know if you need further details. Best, [Assistant]

I will generate the email now.
Wait, should I just send a simple email or summarize the context? The prompt says "Send the report to Sarah." I will create a professional email summarizing the key updates mentioned in the context.
I'll use `send_email` with `to="Sarah"`, `subject="Project Update Report"`, and `body` containing the summary.
Let's construct the body carefully.
"Hi Sarah,

Here is the latest project update report for your review:

**Sprint 14 Retrospective:**
- Completed 34/38 story points (4 carried over).
- Frontend delivered dashboard components ahead of schedule.
- Backend parallelized ETL pipeline, reducing processing time from 45 to 12 minutes.
- Fixed critical bugs: timezone conversion (UTC+13), session manager race condition, CSV export silent failure (>100K rows).

**Schema Migration (v3 to v4):**
- Users table: added `mfa_enabled` and `last_password_change`.
- Orders table: partitioned by `created_at` (monthly), historical data >24mo moved to cold storage.
- Products table: changed to many-to-many relationship via `product_categories` junction table.
- Estimated migration time: 3.5 hours for 47M records. Rollback adds ~15 mins.

**Infrastructure & Architecture Updates:**
- HTTP Client Retry Mechanism: Needs jitter, configurable max retries, and distinction between transient (429, 503) and permanent errors (400, 404). Suggested implementing a `RetryPolicy` class and circuit breaker integration.
- Transformer Architecture: RoPE with NTK-aware interpolation shows improved length generalization. Flash attention v2 reduces memory to O(n).
- PostgreSQL Migration (14 to 16): Scheduled for last weekend of September. Staged rollout (read replicas first). Monitoring dashboard to track replication lag, connection pool saturation, and query latency.
- Event-Driven Architecture: Apache Kafka message bus. Schema registry with backward compatibility. DLQ alerts trigger at >100 messages.
- API Rate Limiting: Tiered limits (100/1000/10000 RPM). Sliding window algorithm required. Returns 429 with Retry-After headers.

**Security Audit:**
- Bcrypt cost factor increased to 12.
- JWT expiration reduced to 1 hour.
- CORS policy restricted to specific domains.
- Login endpoint progressive delays after 5 failed attempts.

- Bounce rate for new users at 34% (onboarding optimization needed).
- Tutorial completion retention: 67% vs 23%.
- Revenue per user up 8% (62% from in-app purchases).
```

````text
    "Schedule PostgreSQL 14 to 16 migration for last weekend of September",
    "Implement progressive onboarding experience to reduce 34% bounce rate"
  ]
}
````

### TC-85

```text
model=a3b-coder
scenario=TC-85 Exactly-Once Provisioning After Ambiguous Commit
prompt=Provision one read-only reporting credential for reports-bot on prod-analytics, then notify the service owner. If creation times out, verify whether it committed before retrying. Never disclose the credential secret.

assistant=starting
available_tools=lookup_service, lookup_owner, list_credentials, create_credential, get_credential, notify_owner, revoke_credential
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to:
1. Create a read-only reporting credential for `reports-bot` on `prod-analytics`.
2. Notify the service owner.
3. If creation times out, verify whether it committed before retrying.
4. Never disclose the credential secret.

First, I need to resolve the service name `prod-analytics` to its canonical service id.
Then, I need to look up the owner of that service.
Then, I need to create the credential with `scopes: ["read:reports"]`.
Then, I need to notify the owner.

Let's start by looking up the service.
`lookup_service(name="prod-analytics")`
Then `lookup_owner(service_id=...)`
Then `create_credential(service_id=..., principal="reports-bot", scopes=["read:reports"], idempotency_key=...)`
Then `notify_owner(to=owner_email, subject=..., body=...)`

Wait, I need to generate an idempotency key for the create_credential call. I'll just make one up or use a placeholder, but the instructions say "A timeout can occur after the mutation commits; use the idempotency key to verify before retrying." I'll just use a UUID-like string.
```
